# Easywurld — 30 Day Social Plan
### 11 core posts a day, restructured for TikTok, Instagram, Facebook and LinkedIn

**Every post is ready to publish.** Hook, beats, voiceover, slide copy, caption, hashtags and
visual direction are all written. No placeholders, nothing to decide on the day.

| | |
|---|---|
| Plan starts | **Monday 19 October 2026** (change it in the app if you want a different month) |
| Ends | **Tuesday 17 November 2026** |
| Daily volume | 11 core posts → 11 TikTok + 11 Instagram + 11 Facebook + 4 LinkedIn (+1 optional) = **37 posts a day** |
| Month total | **1,140 posts** (1,110 scheduled + 30 optional) |
| Times | Africa/Lagos (WAT, UTC+1). Every post block carries its own time. |
| Phone tool | `EASYWURLD_POST_COMMANDER.html` — one file, works offline, copy button on every block, tick marks that save on the phone. |
| Spreadsheet | `content_calendar.csv` — one row per post, dated, editable on your phone in Google Sheets. |
| Brand rules | `marketing/brand-prompt.md`. Never invent results, ratings or testimonials. |

**How to read a post block.** Each post has one core idea and up to four platform versions.
The platform versions are different writing, not the same caption pasted four times.

**The daily mix.** 3 teaching, 2 memes, 2 trust posts, 2 offer posts, 1 story, 1 hot take.
Memes sit near 20% of output.

**Scheduling a month ahead.** Everything below is dated. Open the sheet, sort by date, and
schedule the whole month in one sitting. The app is for the days when something needs
writing on the spot.

---

## Post 01 · The three-part price reply

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Relief. It gives them the exact fix for a moment they have already lived through today.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Kinetic type, 20s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Someone asked how much. You sent the number. They vanished.

**On-screen beats**
- The problem is not the price
- 1 · Say the price
- 2 · Say what it includes (one line)
- 3 · Ask one question that moves it forward
- That question is the whole trick

**Voiceover script**
> Someone asked how much, you sent the number, and the chat went quiet. The problem is not the price. It is what happened after it. Try three parts. Say the price. Say what it includes in one line. Then ask one question that moves things forward. That question is what turns a number into a conversation. I keep 36 of these scripts in one file. Five thousand naira, details on my page.

**Caption**
> Price + what it includes + one question.
> 
> That is the whole trick. A price on its own is a dead end.
> 
> 36 scripts like this in the Chat-to-Customer Playbook. ₦5,000. Message PLAYBOOK on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessNigeria #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #WhatsAppBusiness #CustomerServiceTips

**Visual:** Cream background, four lines arriving one at a time, one terracotta tick on the last line.

### Instagram · 07:30 · 5-slide carousel, 4:5

**Slide copy**
1. You sent the price. They disappeared.
2. 1 · Say the price
3. 2 · Say what it includes (one line is enough)
4. 3 · Ask one question that moves it forward
5. Price + scope + question = a conversation, not a dead end

**Caption**
> A price on its own is a dead end. A price with a next step is a conversation.
> 
> Try the three-part reply on your next enquiry and watch what changes.
> 
> The Chat-to-Customer Playbook has 36 scripts like this, plus a tracker so no follow-up gets forgotten. ₦5,000, you use it yourself. Message PLAYBOOK on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837.

**Hashtags:** #WhatsAppBusiness #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #Copywriting

**Visual:** Same cream palette, one terracotta tick on slide 5, mark bottom right.

### Facebook · 08:00 · Feed post

**On-screen beats**
- It will not create customers
- It will not fix a price that is too high
- It will not make you reply at 2am
- It will not guarantee sales
- What it does: stop you losing the ones you already have

## Post 05 · Set up WhatsApp Business properly in four minutes

**Type:** How-to · **Why it works:** Competence. Something they can finish before their next customer arrives.

### TikTok · 15:00 · Screen recording or hand-held demo, 30s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Your WhatsApp profile is doing nothing for you right now.

**On-screen beats**
- 1 · Put a real photo, not a logo
- 2 · Name it what you do
- 3 · Add your services to the catalogue
- 4 · Turn on greeting and away messages
- 5 · Save your best 5 replies as quick replies

**Voiceover script**
> Four minutes. Open WhatsApp Business, not plain WhatsApp. First, put a real photo there, people buy from faces, not logos. Second, name it what you do, not your initials. Third, add your services to the catalogue so people can tap instead of typing. Fourth, turn on greeting and away messages, and make them sound human. Five, save the five replies you send every day as quick replies. That is the setup. The 7-day checklist does the rest.

**Caption**
> Plain WhatsApp is fine for chatting. WhatsApp Business is for selling.
> 
> 4 minutes of setup: photo, name, catalogue, greeting, quick replies.
> 
> The 7-day setup checklist is inside the Chat-to-Customer Playbook. ₦5,000. Message PLAYBOOK on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837

### LinkedIn · 17:15 · Text post, 17:15

> 9:47pm. The shop closed at seven.
> 
> Three people asked about the same thing today, so I wrote the same answer three times. If the wording was slightly off, I will write it slightly differently tomorrow. That loop is where a small business quietly loses its owner.
>

## Post 05 · Ask for a review in a way that does not make you cringe

## Post 09 · Before you post, run this five point check

**Type:** Checklist · **Why it works:** Control. A pre-flight check that makes a bad post unlikely.

### TikTok · 20:00 · Kinetic checklist, 22s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Run this before every post. Two minutes.

**On-screen beats**
- 1 · Would I say this to a customer face to face?
- 2 · Does it say what you do, or only that you exist?
- 3 · Is there one thing they can do next?
- 4 · Did I remove the sentence that repeats the headline?
- 5 · Is there a number, a name or a real detail?

**Voiceover script**
> Run this before every post, two minutes. One, would I say this to a customer face to face. Two, does it say what I do, or only that I exist. Three, is there one thing they can do next. Four, did I remove the sentence that repeats the headline. Five, is there a number, a name or a real detail in it. If you can answer all five, post it. If not, you have one edit to make and it will be better than ninety percent of what is on the feed.

> Two minutes before every post. Five questions.

> If you can answer all five, post it.

> It is about the person reading it. Their problem, at eight in the evening, on a Tuesday, with about four minutes of patience left.
> 
> Not your journey. Not your values. Not your vision. Your business is the solution, not the topic.
> 
> So before you post, ask one question: what does this person need to know today? Then answer that, clearly, and let your business be the obvious place they go for the rest.
> 
> Most small business accounts do not have a posting problem. They have a subject problem. They are posting about the business because the business is what they know.
> 
> The 30-Day Content Engine is 30 days of prompts built around the reader instead, ₦5,000.
>

> 2. The promise is in the first line, not in the button
> 3. The price is visible, not 'contact us for pricing'
> 4. One button, and you have clicked it yourself
> 5. Payment details are on the page so nobody is waiting
> 6. You know exactly what happens after the click
> 7. Somebody else can read it and understand it
> 
> Number seven is the one people skip. If you have to explain it to your mother, it is not clear enough. If a friend has to ask you a question before they can act, you have lost them.
> 
> The One-Offer Landing Page Kit has this checklist with instructions, plus the structure and the copy templates. ₦5,000.

## Post 01 · The three questions your message must answer

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Clarity. A simple structure they can use on anything they write.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Kinetic type, 20s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Your message only has to answer three questions.

**On-screen beats**
- What do you do?
- Who is it for?
- What should they do next?
- If one is missing, they leave
- That is the whole message

**Voiceover script**
> Your message only has to answer three questions. What do you do. Who is it for. What should they do next. If one of those is missing, the person leaves, and they leave quietly, so you never find out. That is the whole message. Everything else is decoration around it, and decoration does not get paid for.

**Caption**
> What do you do. Who is it for. What next.
> 
> If one is missing, they leave quietly.

## Post 05 · Turn a vague offer into a specific sentence

**Type:** How-to · **Why it works:** Craft. Shows the actual edit, which is the part people never get taught.

### TikTok · 15:00 · Kinetic type, 25s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Here is how to turn a vague offer into a sentence people remember.

**On-screen beats**
- Vague: 'I help businesses grow online'
- Step 1: name the person
- 'I help food businesses in Lagos'
- Step 2: name the result, not the activity
- 'get a site that brings orders'

**Voiceover script**
> Here is how to turn a vague offer into a sentence people can remember. Start with 'I help businesses grow online'. Step one, name the person, not the market. I help food businesses in Lagos. Step two, name the result, not the activity. Not I build websites, but get a site that brings orders. Step three, say how it happens in five words. That is your sentence. Write it, say it, and see if anyone repeats it back to you.

**Caption**
> Name the person. Name the result. Say how in five words.
> 
> Then say it out loud and see if anybody repeats it back.

- So the scope kept moving

## Post 01 · What to ask an AI assistant so it does not give you generic nonsense

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Control over a tool they already use badly. Very practical, immediately usable.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Kinetic type, 22s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Your AI assistant is not the problem. Your prompt is.

**On-screen beats**
- Do not say: 'write me a caption'
- Do say: 'write a caption for a Lagos bakery, warm, 2 lines, no emoji'
- Give it: who, where, tone, length, what to avoid
- Paste a real example of your voice
- Then ask for 3 options, not 1

**Voiceover script**
> Your AI assistant is not the problem, your prompt is. Do not say, write me a caption. Do say, write a caption for a Lagos bakery, warm tone, two lines, no emoji. Give it who it is for, where they are, the tone, the length, and what to avoid. Paste a real example of how you write. And ask for three options, not one, because the first one is never the one you wanted.

**Caption**
> Give it: who, where, tone, length, what to avoid.
> Then ask for three, not one.

**Hashtags:** #AIforBusiness #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #ProductivityTips #SmallBusinessNigeria

**Visual:** Cream, bad prompt replaced by good prompt, terracotta on the good one.

3. Instead: 'caption for a Lagos bakery, warm, 2 lines, no emoji'
4. Give it: who, where, tone, length, what to avoid
5. Then ask for 3 options, not 1

**Caption**
> Also paste one real example of your own writing, so it learns your voice instead of the internet's.
> 
> The Everyday AI Prompt Desk is 50 prompts written for small businesses: replies, product descriptions, proposals, posts, complaints, follow-ups, and the awkward ones. ₦5,000. Message PROMPTS on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837.

### Facebook · 08:00 · Feed post

> Your AI assistant is not the problem. Your prompt is.
> 
> Do not say: 'write me a caption.'
> 
> Say: 'write a caption for a Lagos bakery, warm tone, two lines, no emoji, mention Saturday deliveries.'
> 
> Give it five things: who it is for, where they are, the tone, the length, and what to avoid. Then paste one real example of how you write, so it learns your voice and not the internet's.
> 
> And ask for three options, not one. The first option is never the one you wanted, and if you only asked for one you will spend longer editing than writing.
> 
> The Everyday AI Prompt Desk has 50 of these, written for small businesses, including the awkward ones. ₦5,000.
> 
> Message PROMPTS on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837

### LinkedIn · 08:15 · Text post, 08:15

> Most complaints about AI writing are actually complaints about prompting.
> 
> 'Write me a caption' returns the average of the internet. It has no idea who you are, who you are writing to, what you sell, how long the answer should be, or what you have decided you never want to say again.
> 
> Give it five things instead: audience, location, tone, length, and what to avoid. Then paste one genuine example of your own writing, because a single real sample teaches voice better than three paragraphs of instruction.
> 
> Ask for three options, not one. The first answer is a rough guess, and giving yourself options is how you keep your own judgement in the loop instead of accepting the machine's first move.
> 
> We wrote 50 of these prompts for small businesses: replies, descriptions, proposals, posts, complaints, follow-ups. The Everyday AI Prompt Desk is ₦5,000.

> WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837. Message PROMPTS.

## Post 01 · How to sound credible before you have results to show

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Relief. A lot of small businesses think trust only comes from testimonials. It comes from being specific.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Kinetic type with voiceover, 25s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> No testimonials yet? You are not behind. You are just using the wrong proof.

**On-screen beats**
- Proof is not only results
- It can be how you think
- Show the constraint, not the boast
- Say what you will not do
- That reads as honest

**Voiceover script**
> Most small businesses think trust comes from testimonials. It does not. It comes from being specific enough to check. I have built pages with no client results to show. What I showed instead was the thinking. Here are four things you can put on a page tomorrow, with nothing to invent. One: the constraint. Say who it is not for. Two: the decision behind your work. Three: the honest limitation. Four: the process, step by step. Pick one. Put it on the page. You do not need a famous name to be believed, you need to be checkable.

**Caption**
> Trust is not only results.
> 
> If you have no testimonials yet, show the thinking instead. The constraint, the decision, the honest limit, the process.
> 
> Be specific enough to be checked. That is what trust is made of.

## Post 03 · We will not use a number we cannot evidence

**Type:** Trust · **Why it works:** Trust. Saying out loud what you refuse to do is the fastest way to sound credible to a careful buyer.

### TikTok · 11:30 · Talking head, 30s

**Hook (0-3s)**

**On-screen beats**
- "Trusted by 200+ businesses"
- We have not served 200 businesses
- We have built 49 sample projects
- And we label every one of them a sample
- That is the whole trick

**Voiceover script**
> Here is something we will never put on our own page. Trusted by two hundred plus businesses. We have not served two hundred businesses. We have built forty-nine sample projects, and every single one of them is labelled as a sample on our site. People think modesty is a personality trait. It is not. It is a business trait. When you say no to a number you cannot evidence, everything else you say gets easier to believe.

> 
> We have built 49 concept projects. Every one of them says so on the site.

> Saying no to a number you cannot evidence is a business trait, not a personality trait.

> We have never written "trusted by X businesses" on our own site, and we have labelled all 49 of our portfolio builds as concept projects or sample builds, on the page itself.
> 
> That is not humility. It is a decision about what a claim is worth.
> 
> A claim is only useful if the buyer can check it. "Trusted by 200+" cannot be checked. It can only be admired, and admiration is where trust goes to die.
> 
> If you run a business, the version of this is simple: every number on your page should survive one question. Who is this? Where is the evidence? Can I check it? If the answer is no, it is decoration, and decoration costs you credibility when it is found out.
> 
> The same rule applies to the work itself. We would rather look smaller and be believed than look bigger and be doubted.
> 
> WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837 if you want a free 24-hour review of your page.

## Post 09 · What a sample project taught us about real customers

**Type:** Story · **Why it works:** Story. A specific moment from the work, honestly framed, builds trust without any fake proof.

**On-screen beats**
- It was the menu
- Forty items, no prices, no photos
- We had to invent all of it
- And that made the design honest

**Voiceover script**
> We built a sample restaurant site recently. The hardest part was not the design, and it was not the code. It was the menu. A real restaurant has forty items, half of them with photos taken on a phone in bad light, and nobody has written the prices down anywhere. For our sample build we had to invent every item, every price, every description, and that constraint is what made the design honest. If we had been building it for a real restaurant, that conversation would have been the first week of work, not a detail.

**Caption**
> The hardest part of our sample restaurant site was the menu.
> 
> Not the design. The menu. Forty items, no prices, no photos. We invented all of it, and that is what made the design honest.

## Post 01 · Service one: what digital marketing strategy actually is

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Teach. It answers the question people ask most and cannot define: what does a marketing plan even contain.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Kinetic type with voiceover, 30s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> What is a marketing strategy, really? Here is the honest version.

**On-screen beats**
- It is a set of decisions
- Who you are for
- What you say to them
- Where you say it
- What happens after

**Voiceover script**
> What is a marketing strategy, really? Here is the honest version. It is a set of decisions. Who you are for. What you say to them. Where you say it. What happens after they respond. That is it. It is not a mood board and it is not a calendar with pictures in it. If a document does not answer those four questions, it is decoration. And if you cannot write the first two without guessing, that is the real problem, and no amount of posting fixes it.

**Caption**
> A marketing strategy is four decisions:
> 
> 1 · Who you are for
> 2 · What you say to them
> 3 · Where you say it
> 4 · What happens after they reply
> 
> If a document does not answer those, it is decoration.

**Hashtags:** #DigitalMarketing #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld #Strategy

**Visual:** Cream background, four numbered lines, terracotta tick on the last one.

### Instagram · 07:30 · 5-slide carousel, 4:5

**Slide copy**
1. What is a marketing strategy, really?
2. It is a set of decisions.

## Post 01 · How to set a price without apologising for it

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Teach. Naming the three parts of a price answer gives them language for the next enquiry today.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Kinetic type with voiceover, 25s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> You should not apologise when you give a price. Here is the three-part answer.

**On-screen beats**
- 1 · The number
- 2 · What it includes
- 3 · The next step
- No apologising, no justifying

**Voiceover script**
> You should not apologise when you give a price. Not sorry, not just, not the price is a bit. Three parts: the number, one line on what it includes, and the next step. And no justifying. The moment you explain the price, you tell them it is too high. Just say it, say what is inside, and ask the question that keeps the conversation alive.

**Caption**
> Never apologise when you give a price.
> 
> 1 · The number
> 2 · What it includes, one line
> 3 · The next step
> 
> The moment you justify a price, you tell the customer it is too high.

**Caption**
> The Clear Message Workbook has 30 message templates for the moments where you have to say something out loud: the price, the delay, the no, the apology. ₦5,000, message MESSAGE on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837.

**Hashtags:** #PricingTips #Easywurld #LagosSME #Copywriting

### Facebook · 08:00 · Feed post

> Never apologise when you give a price.
> 
> Not "sorry, it is a bit". Not "the price is 45,000 but". Not "it is quite expensive but".
> 
> Three parts, in this order:
> 
> 1. The number.
> 2. One line on what it includes.
> 3. The next step.
> 
> "The Ankara set is ₦18,000. It is tailored to your size and ready in five days. Would you like me to take your measurements now?"
> 
> The moment you start justifying a price, you have told the customer it is too high. Saying the number, saying what is inside, and asking a question takes five seconds and does not argue with anyone.
> 
> The Clear Message Workbook, ₦5,000, has 30 templates for the moments where you have to say something out loud. Message MESSAGE on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837.

### LinkedIn · 08:15 · Text post, 08:15

> A small habit with a large effect: stop apologising when you quote.
> 
> "Sorry, it is 45,000 but..." has started a sentence in a way that is already working against you. The word "but" tells the buyer to look for the reason the number is wrong.
> 
> Three parts instead:
> 
> The number. One line on what it includes. The next step.
> 
> "The Ankara set is ₦18,000. Tailored to your size, ready in five days. Shall I take your measurements?"
> 
> The reason this works is not confidence theatre. Apologising adds information the buyer did not ask for: that the price is at the edge of what you consider acceptable. Once they know that, they anchor to it.
> 
> We put 30 versions of these, for prices, delays, difficult no's and apologies, into the Clear Message Workbook, ₦5,000. It does not write the messages for you. It gives you the sentences so you stop starting from a blank page at 11pm.
> 
> WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837. Message MESSAGE.

## Post 03 · When to raise your price, and how to say it

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Teach. Raising a price is a fear for small business owners, and the trigger point settles it.

### TikTok · 11:30 · Kinetic type with voiceover, 30s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Three signs you should raise your price. Most businesses need this yesterday.

**On-screen beats**
- 1 · You are turning work away
- 2 · You dread the quote
- 3 · Your last three jobs took longer
- Raise it small, then raise it again

**Voiceover script**
> Three signs you should raise your price. One, you are turning work away. Two, you dread writing the quote. Three, your last three jobs took longer than they should. Any one of those and your price is wrong. When you do raise it, do not double it. Raise it by twenty percent on new work only, keep the old price for old clients, and say nothing about it. The increase is not a speech. Nobody is owed an explanation for your price.

**Caption**
> Three signs it is time to raise your price:
> 
> 1 · You are turning work away
> 2 · You dread writing the quote
> 3 · The last three jobs took too long
> 
> Raise it 20%. New work only. Say nothing.

## Post 07 · What a customer is actually comparing your price to

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Teach. It reframes price objections as a comparison problem, which is a genuinely new angle.

### TikTok · 18:00 · Kinetic type with voiceover, 30s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Your customer is not comparing your price to your cost. They are comparing it to their last bad experience.

**On-screen beats**
- They remember the last one
- Not the last good one
- That is why clarity beats cheap
- Tell them what they avoid

**Voiceover script**
> Your customer is not comparing your price to your cost. They are comparing it to their last bad experience, and unfortunately the last bad experience is more memorable than the last good one. That is why clarity beats cheap. A slightly higher price with a clear explanation of what they are avoiding is usually easier to accept than a lower price with silence. Tell them what goes wrong, not what goes right.

**Caption**
> Your customer is not comparing your price to your cost.
> 
> They are comparing it to their last bad experience, and the bad one sticks.
> 
> That is why clarity beats cheap. Tell them what they avoid.

> A pricing idea worth trying: sell the avoidance, not the promise.
> 
> A buyer is not comparing your price to your costs. They are comparing it to their last bad experience, and bad experiences are more memorable than good ones. That memory is the reference point, and it is not a number, it is a feeling.
> 
> So a slightly higher price with a clear statement of what they are avoiding is often easier to accept than a lower price with silence. The buyer is not paying for the feature list, they are paying for the feeling of not repeating a bad month.
> 
> Practical version for a small business: in your quote reply, after the price, name the thing that usually goes wrong in this kind of job and that you handle. One line. Not a guarantee, not a claim you cannot evidence, just the thing you know from experience.
> 
> We put 30 templates for moments like this in the Clear Message Workbook, ₦5,000.
> 
> WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837. Message MESSAGE.

## Post 11 · The one question to ask before you lower your price

**Type:** Direct response · **Why it works:** Direct response. A practical question the audience can ask in their next conversation, which drives comments and saves.

### TikTok · 21:30 · Kinetic type with voiceover, 20s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Before you drop your price, ask this one question.

**On-screen beats**
- "What were you hoping to pay?"
- If they say a number,
price is not the problem
- If they say nothing,
they have no comparison
- Now you know what to fix

**Voiceover script**
> Before you drop your price, ask this one question: what were you hoping to pay? If they give you a number, the price is not the problem, and you can either meet it or walk. If they say nothing, they have no comparison, and the problem is that they do not yet understand what they are getting. Those are two completely different problems and they need two completely different answers. One question tells you which one you have.

**Caption**
> Before you drop your price, ask: what were you hoping to pay?
> 
> A number means price is not the problem.
> Silence means they have no comparison yet.
> 
> One question. Two different fixes.

> A spreadsheet where every enquiry is logged, with a status and a next follow-up date. It does two jobs, and the second one matters more:

> It tells you who to contact today. And it tells you who to stop chasing.
> 
> The second job protects the business. Chasing past the third follow-up trains customers to ignore your number, and the next enquiry gets ignored too. A tracker makes that visible, which is the only way to change it.
> 
> Alongside it: the enquiry-to-order flow written down, 36 scripts with notes on when each one is wrong to use, and a 7-day setup checklist.

> ₦5,000, digital download, no subscription. It does not generate leads. It handles the ones you already have, which is where most of the money is being lost.

## Post 08 · The customer who says call me

### TikTok · 19:00 · Static meme, 12s, with voiceover

**Voiceover script**
> Customer: call me. Me: I am at the shop. Customer: okay, call me. Me: when are you free? Customer: also call me. This is the most common sentence in Nigerian small business and it is the easiest one to fix. Give them two time slots. Not a range. Two slots. A person can reply to two slots, a person cannot reply to a range.

**Caption**

> Customer: okay, call me.
> Customer: also call me.
> 
> The fix: give two time slots, not a range. Nobody can reply to a range.

- We cut it to 1
- Enquiries doubled in a fortnight
- Then we changed nothing else
- So we knew it was that

If we had also changed the prices and the posts in the same week, we would have learned nothing. One change at a time, or you learn nothing from any of it.

- Say what is not included

It is more professional, and it is faster to agree.

## Post 10 · A proposal is not a price list

**Type:** Hot take · **Why it works:** Hot take. It reframes a boring document into a persuasion tool, and it is a position people disagree with.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 25s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> A proposal is not a price list. I will explain what that means.

**On-screen beats**
- A price list says: this costs X
- A proposal says: here is
what I understand, and here
is what happens next
- Then it says: this costs X
- Same price, different outcome

**Voiceover script**
> A proposal is not a price list. A price list says this costs X. A proposal says here is what I understand about your problem, here is what I am going to do about it, here is what I need from you, and this is what it costs. Same price, completely different outcome. The customer is not comparing your price to your cost. They are comparing your document to the alternative of doing nothing, which is free, and which is why the document has to do some work.

**Caption**
> A proposal is not a price list.
> 
> A price list says: this costs X.
> A proposal says: here is what I understand, here is what happens next, and this is what it costs.
> 
> Same price. Different outcome.

**Hashtags:** #HotTake #Easywurld #BusinessTips #SmallBusinessNigeria

## Post 01 · What a small business day actually looks like

one customer, one complaint
- 2pm · the actual work
- 6pm · close, and start again
- That is why content is hard

**Voiceover script**
> A small business day is not what people think. Here is a real one. Eight in the morning, open, deliveries, an argument about something. Eleven, three WhatsApp chats, one customer, one complaint. Two in the afternoon, the actual work. Six in the evening, close, and then the admin you promised yourself you would do. That is why content is hard. Not because people are lazy. Because there is no time left by the time everybody has stopped asking.

> 8am · open, deliveries, an argument

> 2pm · the actual work

> 11am: three chats, one customer, one complaint.
> 2pm: the actual work.
> 6pm: close, and then the admin you promised yourself.
> 
> That is why content is hard. Not laziness. By the time everyone has stopped asking, there is no time left, and a plan that assumes two spare hours a day is a plan that fails in week two.
> 
> Worse, when it fails, the owner usually concludes that they are bad at marketing. They are not. The plan was written for a business with a marketing department and a business with a marketing department in it.
> 
> If you want to post regularly with the hours you have, plan around the time you actually have. A mix that can be produced in fifteen minutes on a bad day, and expanded on a good one, will survive contact with December.
> 
> The 30-Day Content Engine, ₦5,000, is built that way.
> 
> WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837. Message ENGINE.

## Post 04 · What we do when the answer is no

the part that failed

**Voiceover script**

> 5. A one-page prompt card you can print and keep by the phone.
> 
> It is a desk, not a subscription. Nothing renews, nothing is emailed to you.
> 
> Message PROMPTS on WhatsApp: 0905 069 0837.

# How to publish this from your phone

**One file.** `EASYWURLD_POST_COMMANDER.html` is a single file, works with no internet, and
saves your tick marks on the phone. Add it to your home screen and it behaves like an app.

**Free scheduling, no new apps.**

| Platform | How to schedule | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Instagram + Facebook | Meta Business Suite, timezone Africa/Lagos | Free |
| TikTok | Schedule option inside the app for business accounts | Free |
| LinkedIn | The schedule button in the post composer | Free |

**Batch it on Sunday night, 20 minutes.** Schedule the week, then leave the two slots that
need a real human reply open. Do not schedule a post you have not read out loud once.

**Storage.** Keep images and videos in Google Drive, not on the phone. Open them from Drive
when you need them. Text is cheap, video is not.

**Reply first, post second.** A reply to a real customer beats one more post. If a slot
arrives and a customer is waiting, answer the customer and let the post wait.
